宏观研究|宏观跟踪报告

[Table_Title] 
经济结构分化持续，政策有待逐步落地

[Table_ReportType] 
——10月经济数据跟踪

[Table_ReportDate] 2026年11月16日

[Table_Summary] ⚫ 事件：2026年10月，工业增加值当月同比增速回落 0.7个百分
点至4.8%，环比上涨0.21%。固投累计同比由-7.2%降至-8.1%，
制造业、基建、房地产投资分别由-2.1%、-4.2%、-20.6%下降为-
2.9%、-5.1%、-21.6%。社零当月同比为0.4%，前值为 1.1%。 
⚫ 工业生产回落，高技术产业再度上行。随十一、中秋节前“抢生
产”效应结束，工业生产季节性回落。产销率同比放缓，发电量
同比持续下滑，工业企业出口保持旺盛。采矿业、制造业增速放
缓，高技术产业再度上行至高位，为工业生产的重要支撑。上游
原材料行业生成整体边际走弱，能化产业链受油价影响增速放
缓，中游设备制造业生产韧性仍强，下游汽车生产持续下跌，AI
相关产品生产保持旺盛，消费品制造业增速整体上行。服务业生
产、商务活动指数回落，服务业活动预期指数小幅下滑。 
⚫ 投资分项全面下滑，地产投资拖累持续。地产投资增速持续下
滑，新开工增速小幅反弹，施工、竣工增速跌幅加深。地产销售
量价齐跌，去库进程持续，二手房销售保持旺盛。三四线城市新
建住宅价格持续承压，房价尚未企稳，房地产“银十”行情表现
偏弱，政策对市场提振效果有限。房企资金来源持续收紧，自筹
资金和国内贷款渠道延续承压，二手房销售给按揭贷款带来支
撑。上月政府集中推地带动下，房企拿地好转。但去库周期和预
售资金制度改革影响，房企资金周转约束持续，拿地进程预期持
续偏缓。 
⚫ 基建增速持续放缓，资金形成实物工作量相对滞后。10月基建投
资下行斜率变陡，随着专项债发行、新型政策性金融工具投放完
成，资金投放进程边际提速，但财政资金拨付到形成实物工作量
仍需时间，未来仍有充足的改善动能。 
⚫ 制造业投资增速回落，高技术产业增速放缓，出口拉动仍强。传
统行业盈利偏弱，制约了企业自主扩产意愿。海外需求保持韧
性，带动了相关行业需求。设备工器具购置同比增速由 9.7%回落
为 8.9%，大规模设备更新政策资金在 7月下达完毕，随着资金逐
步落地，政策效果边际减弱。 
⚫ 消费增速下跌，内需依然偏弱。假期出行前置，部分消费需求提
前释放给10月消费带来一定透支作用，高基数效应和价格波动抑
制了消费意愿。餐饮、商品零售均回落，网上零售增速下滑。可
选消费多数回落，石油及制品类上行幅度最大，通讯器材类跌幅
相对最大，金银珠宝类同比增速回落，金价10月继续下跌，消费
者观望情绪影响下，推迟了购买决策，中秋假期错位至 9月也使
得部分需求提前透支。地产下游行业中，家电和家具类增速小幅
上行，主要受低基数拉动，建材增速继续下滑。必选消费集体走
弱，其中，烟酒类跌幅较大，9月提前备货带来一定影响。

事件 
2026年10月，工业增加值当月同比增速回落0.7个百分点至4.8%（市场预期5.3%），环
比上涨0.21%，前值为0.61%。1-10月，固定资产投资累计同比增速-8.1%（市场预期
-6.9%），前值-7.2%，其中，房地产开发投资累计同比增速回落1个百分点至-21.6%，
基建投资累计同比增速回落0.9个百分点至-5.1%，制造业投资增速累计同比回落0.8
个百分点至-2.9%。社会消费品零售总额当月同比增速由上月的1.1%回落为0.4%（市
场预期1.1%）。

1    工业生产回落，结构分化依然显著 
工业生产季节性回落，下滑幅度超出市场预期，产销率同比放缓，发电量同比持续下
滑，工业企业出口仍有支撑。10月，工业增加值当月同比增速回落0.7个百分点至4.8%，
低于市场预期的5.3%，环比上涨0.21%，前值为0.61%。随十一、中秋节前“抢生产”
效应结束，工业生产季节性回落。发电量同比由上月的-1.2%回落至-2.1%。工业企业
出口交货值当月同比由上月的17%继续上行至18.6%，海外制造业景气度较高，价格上
涨，原油、AI产业链拉动下，出口仍是工业生产重要拉动项。规模以上工业企业产销
率当月同比由-0.2%下行为-0.4%。 
采矿业、制造业增速放缓，高技术产业再度上行至高位，为工业生产的重要支撑。分
大类看，10月，采矿业同比回落0.6个百分点至0.7%，石油和天然气开采业同比高位
小幅放缓，高油价部分压制了石化产业链生产。制造业同比由上月的6%回落至5.4%；
高技术产业同比由上月的15.3%上行至17.6%，装备制造业同比增速为10.6%，持续领
跑制造业整体表现，传统制造业生产相对偏弱。电力、燃气及水的生产和供应业同比
回落1.2个百分点至4.4%。

水泥产量同比跌幅收窄，地产对上游产业链拖累作用持续。有色金属冶炼及压延工业
同比跌幅收窄。能化产业链来看，化学原料及化学制品制造业同比由2.4%回落至1.3%，
乙烯产量同比回落，原油价格高位给产业链带来的影响预期仍将持续；化学纤维产量
同比跌幅扩大，下游纺织业增速同比回落1.2个百分点至2.0%。制造业中游生产整体
保持韧性，通用设备、电气机械及器材制造业增速与上月持平，专用设备制造业同比
边际放缓，整体生产处于高位。下游制造业中，汽车制造业同比大幅下跌5.8个百分
点至0.9%，本月跌幅相对最大，汽车产量同比跌幅加深，SUV产量下跌为主要拖累。
计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业同比由16.4%上行至19.4%，本月上行幅度相对最
大，集成电路产量同比由18.4%上行至21.9%，为电子计算机产量同比跌幅加深，工业
机器人产量同比走高。铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业同比回落2.5个
百分点至9.2%。消费品制造业中，医药制造业同比上行1个百分点至5.9%，食品制造
业增速上行，农副食品加工业增速小幅回落。

2    投资：固投分项全面下滑，设备购置增速回落 
固定资产投资分项全部下滑，地产、基建、制造业增速同步回落，资金向实物工作量
转化滞后，企业扩产意愿不足。2026年10月，固定资产投资累计同比增速-8.1%，市
场预期-6.9%，前值-7.2%，固投季调环比增速为-2.93%，前值为0.85%。其中，房地
产开发投资累计同比增速回落1个百分点至-21.6%，增速持续下滑；基建累计同比增
速回落0.9个百分点至-5.1%，制造业累计同比增速回落0.8个百分点至-2.9%，民间固
定资产投资回落1个百分点至-11.4%。第一产业固定投资累计同比下滑幅度最大，第
二、三产业同比跌幅持续加深。设备工器具购置同比增速由9.7%回落为8.9%；建筑安
装工程同比由-10%回落为-11%，新开工偏弱的影响持续。

自2026年10月1日起实施，优惠政策主要针对刚需住房，房地产“银十”行情表现偏
弱，政策对市场提振效果有限。居民消费信心偏弱，加杠杆仍然偏谨慎，预期年内地
产销售延续低位。

图表8：房屋竣工、施工增速继续下滑（%） 图表9：商品房销售额、面积同比下跌（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

房企资金来源持续收紧，自筹资金和国内贷款渠道延续承压，二手房销售给按揭贷款
带来支撑。房地产开发资金来源增速由-21.6%进一步回落至-21.9%。其中，自筹资金
和国内贷款延续下行，房企资质偏弱影响下，相关资金来源受阻。定金和预收款同比
由-14.8%回落至-15.5%，新房销售偏弱影响持续，预售资金也受政策调整影响。个人
按揭贷款同比持平于-22.1%，二手房销售交易活跃及前期按揭滞后发放带来支撑。 
上月政府集中推地带动下，房企拿地好转。100大中城市土地成交来看，10月房企拿
地同环比增速均反弹上行，地方政府为完成土地出让收入目标，百城土地供应量在9
月集中放量，企业拿地存在一定滞后效应，上月供应增加在本月成交端兑现。而百城
土地供应量在10月同、环比大幅回落，月度供应量波动仍大，房地产行业去库存进程
下，叠加预售资金制度改革影响，房企资金周转约束持续，房企拿地进程预期持续偏
缓。

2.2  基建增速持续放缓，资金形成实物工作量相对滞后 
基建投资增速下行斜率变陡，资金形成实物工作量相对滞后。10月基建累计同比增速
回落0.9个百分点至-5.1%，下行速率变陡。随着专项债发行、新型政策性金融工具投
放完成，资金投放进程边际提速，但财政资金拨付到形成实物工作量仍需时间，未来
仍有充足的改善动能。 
基建各分项增速集体回落，水利、环境、公共设施管理业跌幅较大。从细分项来看，
电力、热力、燃气及水的生产和供应业基数逐步走低，但跌幅进一步加深。交通运输、
仓储、邮政业增速由1.9%下跌至0.7%，依然保持正增；水利、环境、公共设施管理业
增速下行1.6个百分点至-14.4%，跌幅相对最大。

图表12：基建投资回落，水泥产量下滑（%） 图表13：水利、环境和公共设施管理业跌幅较大 (%)

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

2.3  制造业增速回落，高技术产业仍为重要支撑 
制造业投资增速放缓，民间投资持续下跌，高技术产业增速放缓，出口拉动仍强。制
造业累计同比增速回落0.8个百分点至-2.9%，民间固定资产投资回落1个百分点至-
11.4%。高技术产业投资累计同比由4.9%回落至4%，增速逐步放缓，但依然为制造业
投资主要拉动项。传统行业盈利偏弱，制约了企业自主扩产意愿。海外需求保持韧性，
价格走高和AI产业链为主要拉动，也带动了相关行业投资分化。设备工器具购置同比
增速由9.7%回落为8.9%，大规模设备更新政策资金在7月下达完毕，随着资金逐步落
地，政策效果边际减弱。后续随着财政资金逐步落地形成实物工作量，有望给制造业
投资带来支撑。

3    消费增速再度放缓，服务、商品消费均回落 
消费增速再度放缓，内需依然偏弱，餐饮、商品零售均回落，网上零售增速下滑。10
月，社会消费品零售总额同比增速由上月的1.1%回落为0.4%，市场预期1.1%。假期出
行前置，部分消费需求提前释放给10月消费带来一定透支作用，高基数效应和价格波
动抑制了消费意愿。其中，除汽车以外的消费品零售增速由上月的3.9%下跌至2.5%；
限额以上企业零售同比由上月的-2.1%下跌至-4.9%，大企业经营依然相对承压。餐饮
同比由上月的2.1%下跌至0.7%；商品零售由1%降至0.3%。城镇消费同比增速由0.8%降
至0.2%，乡村消费同比增速由2.7%降至1.5%。商品网上零售累计同比增速由3.8%降至
2.9%，网上商品和服务零售占社零比重逐步回落。从更新到9月的消费者信心数据来
看，消费者信心、收入指数继续回落，消费意愿指数回落幅度相对最大。

图表16：消费增速再度下滑（%） 图表17：网上零售和服务占社零比重逐步回落（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

弱，价格上涨给消费形成压制，消费补贴政策透支也带来一定影响。金银珠宝类同比
增速由-9.6%大幅下行至-28.7%，金价10月继续下跌，消费者观望情绪影响下，推迟
了购买决策，中秋假期错位至9月也使得部分需求提前透支。双十一活动在10月开启，
化妆品增速持续放缓，结合线上消费数据来看，对线上消费拉动效果有限。汽车消费
由-21.2%回落至-21.4%，依然表现偏弱。地产下游行业中，家电和家具类增速小幅上
行，主要受低基数拉动，建材增速继续下滑。必选消费集体走弱，其中，烟酒类跌幅
较大，由13.8%下滑至2.5%，9月提前备货带来一定影响；粮油、食品、饮料、中西药
品类增速均放缓，日用品、服饰类增速转负。

4    总结与展望 
经济结构分化持续，政策有待进一步落地。10月经济数据整体回落，结构分化持续，
新经济仍是主要拉动，旧经济动能依然偏弱。财政资金投放边际提速，但到位进程依
然偏缓，给经济的支撑效果相对有限。随着政策加速落地形成实物工作量，“六张网”
投资有望逐步上行，推动基建投资修复。年内经济结构分化的特征有望持续，经济有
望逐步企稳，完成全年经济增长目标压力不大。

5    风险提示 
居民购房、消费持续偏弱，地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策推进和落地不及预期。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ AI 投資回報疑慮升溫，資金轉向債市避險：市場對大型科技企業 AI 基礎建設投資規模、融資
需求及未來獲利回收能力的疑慮升溫，導致科技股賣壓擴大，10 月 22 日 Nasdaq 指數下跌
約 2.6%。隨著市場風險偏好下降，避險資金轉向美國公債，10 年期公債殖利率單日下降約 19
個基點，創 2023 年 3 月以來最大單日跌幅，並於 23 日回升至 4.99%。市場關注焦點逐漸由
AI 投資規模轉向資本支出效益，後續大型科技企業財報及現金流表現，將成為評估 AI 產業評

價合理性的重要依據。
◼ 中東衝突推升通膨風險：中東衝突持續升溫，伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地阿拉伯，引
發市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂，推升布蘭特原油價格於 10 月 23 日升至每桶 116.72 美元，單
週上漲約 6.2%，創 2022 年以來最高週收盤水準。能源價格攀升加劇全球通膨壓力，並壓抑
市場風險偏好。
◼ 法國政治動盪加劇財政疑慮，歐洲主權債風險重新受到關注：法國因生活成本上升爆發大規模
抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升
約 10 個基點至 4.88%附近，與德國公債利差擴大至約 144 個基點，創 2012 年以來最高收盤
水準，法股單日下跌約 1.5%，歐元同步走弱。市場擔憂政治不確定性可能削弱財政改革進度，
並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線宜審慎，中期聚焦基本面穩健的 AI 企業：近期 AI 資本支出、融資需求及評價調整

使科技股波動加劇，但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變。隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升，具技術優勢、財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利，而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待。
◼ 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：市場風險偏好降溫時，美國公債仍具一定
避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 服務業 PMI 由 55.4 降至 54.9，連續第 27 個月維持擴張。
商業活動及新訂單雖較前月回落，仍保持穩健成長；就業重返擴張區間，
內需與勞動市場仍具韌性。惟價格指數續升創高，出口與進口動能轉弱，
後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化。 
⚫ 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元，較上月增加 13.7%。
出口 3,152 億美元、進口 4,208 億美元，分別月增 1.4%及 4.3%。出口成
長主要受非貨幣黃金、原油及半導體帶動，進口則以原油、黃金及資本
財增加為主。累計前 8 月貿易逆差 5,570 億美元，較去年同期縮減 19.9%，
惟近期進口增幅高於出口，使逆差再度擴大。 
⚫ 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，AI 投資持續帶動經濟與就業動能，勞動市
場仍具韌性；惟去通膨進展有限，核心服務通膨及成本轉嫁壓力仍存。
全體官員支持升息 1 碼，傾向維持限制性政策，並關注 AI 融資、公債
市場波動及網路安全等金融風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美伊緊張情勢再升溫，川普改口不排除選前對伊朗採取軍事行動，
伊朗則持續尋求重啟談判；同時葉門青年運動頻繁襲擊沙國目標，加劇
中東安全風險。若衝突擴大，恐影響荷姆茲與曼德海峽能源運輸，推升
油氣價格並增添全球經濟下行風險。 
⚫ 美國啟動 337 調查，針對 AI 伺服器垂直供電系統（VPD）相關專利侵
權爭議展開審查，鴻海、台達電、廣達等約 20 家業者列名。由於涉及
AI 伺服器關鍵供電技術，若最終認定侵權，相關產品恐面臨輸美限制，
對供應鏈營運帶來影響。 
短評 
⚫ 美國服務業與內需仍具韌性，AI 投資亦支撐經濟動能，惟貿易逆差擴
大、成本壓力升高及通膨黏著，使聯準會仍需維持偏緊縮立場。加上美
伊與中東局勢升溫，能源價格與金融市場波動風險上升，後續景氣不確
定性仍高。

影響，但主計總處預期 10 月將回落。

一、 美國—美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)為 54.9，較上月
55.4 下降 0.5 個百分點，已連續第 27 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近 12
個月平均值 54.1，服務業景氣仍持續穩健擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 61.7
降至 56.5，新訂單指數由 60.9 降至 59.8，雖較前月回落，但仍維持高檔擴張；就
業指數則由 47.8 回升至 50.1，三個月來首度重返擴張區間。價格指數由 72.6 升
至 74.0，創 2022 年 7 月以來新高。供應商交貨指數由 51.3 升至 53.2，交貨時間
再度延長；庫存指數由 56.7 升至 57.8，企業持續提前備貨以因應未來成本上升風
險。此外，未完成訂單指數升至 56.6，創 2022 年 7 月以來高點，惟新出口訂單
指數由 56.3 大幅降至 46.9，八個月來首度跌入收縮區間，進口指數亦由 56.3 降
至 52.9，外部需求及採購動能均降溫。整體而言，美國服務業景氣仍維持穩健擴
張，內需與就業展現一定韌性，但燃料價格上漲、關稅政策、供應鏈限制及地緣
政治等因素持續推升企業成本，後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化對景氣的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元，較上月修正後 928
億美元增加 127 億美元，增幅 13.7%。當月出口 3,152 億美元，較上月增加 45 億
美元(+1.4%)；進口 4,208 億美元，較上月增加 172 億美元(+4.3%)。其中，商品貿
易逆差擴大至 1,366 億美元，服務貿易順差則小幅增至 310 億美元。出口成長主
要受工業用品及原材料出口增加 63 億美元帶動，其中非貨幣黃金、原油及燃油
出口增幅較為顯著，資本財出口亦受半導體、電腦及電腦周邊設備需求增加所支
撐；進口方面，工業用品及原材料進口增加 91 億美元，資本財進口增加 62 億美
元，其中原油、非貨幣黃金、半導體及工業機械進口增幅較大。累計 2026 年 1 至
8 月，美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 5,570 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少 1,382 億美
元，縮減 19.9%；同期出口成長 11.8%，進口增加 4.4%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡
情況較去年同期有所改善，惟近期進口增幅明顯高於出口，使貿易逆差再度呈現
擴大趨勢。 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)經濟：若干官員認為美國經濟成
長動能有所增強，AI 投資規模與速度持續超乎預期，帶動企業投資與生產力提升；

企業活動則受到企業獲利維持高檔及部分政策因素支持；(2)就業：多數官員表示
勞動市場近期略有改善，就業成長維持穩健，失業率仍處低檔，整體勞動市場大
致符合充分就業狀態，供需風險大致平衡；(3)通膨：官員普遍認為近期去通膨進
展有限，非住房核心服務通膨仍偏高，人力、能源及部分進口成本轉嫁情形仍值
得關注；部分官員擔憂 AI 投資與相關支出擴張，可能推升需求並增加通膨持續
性風險；(4)貨幣政策：鑑於經濟活動持續擴張、勞動市場具韌性且通膨仍高於目
標，全體官員支持升息 1 碼。許多官員認為維持較具限制性的政策立場有助降低
通膨風險，部分官員則認為未來可能仍需進一步緊縮政策，以確保通膨回到目標
水準；(5)金融風險：官員普遍認為金融條件整體仍支持經濟成長，但部分官員關
切 AI 基礎設施快速擴張所衍生的融資風險，以及美國公債市場波動、網路安全
與其他潛在金融穩定風險。 
近期美伊及中東局勢再度升溫，市場對區域衝突擴大的疑慮升高。美國總統
川普由先前宣稱期中選舉前不會攻擊伊朗，改口表示「仍有可能」採取軍事行動，
且美軍已就相關軍事選項進行準備，使美伊對抗風險再度攀升；另一方面，伊朗
雖有意透過斡旋重啟談判，但雙方在核計畫與制裁議題上仍存重大分歧。此外，
葉門青年運動持續升高對沙烏地阿拉伯攻擊，利雅德哈立德國王國際機場一週內
多次遇襲，造成多人死傷，凸顯區域安全風險升高。整體而言，若美伊衝突或代
理人戰事進一步升級，恐衝擊荷姆茲海峽及曼德海峽等能源運輸要道，推升國際
油氣價格、加劇通膨壓力，並增添全球金融市場波動及經濟成長下行風險。 
美國國際貿易委員會(USITC)於 10 月 9 日正式啟動 337 條款調查，針對特定
垂直供電系統(Vertical Power Delivery，VPD)、相關零組件及採用該技術的運算設
備是否涉及專利侵權展開審查。此案由美國電源技術廠商 Vicor Corporation 提出
申訴，主張相關產品侵犯其專利權，並要求發布有限排除令及停止侵權命令，以
限制涉案產品進入美國市場。調查對象涵蓋全球約 20 家企業，包括台灣的鴻海、
台達電、廣達及其美國關係企業，另有中國的立訊精密、工業富聯、德國的英飛
凌等國際大廠列名。 本案聚焦 AI 伺服器及高效能運算領域關鍵的供電架構技術，
該技術有助提升晶片供電效率、降低能源損耗，已廣泛應用於資料中心與 AI 運
算設備。隨著全球 AI 基礎建設投資持續擴大，相關專利布局與授權爭議亦日益
升溫。USITC 指出，此次立案僅代表調查程序正式展開，並不表示已認定侵權，
後續將由行政法官進行聽證及審理，預計於 45 天內訂定調查時程。台灣經濟部

二、歐元區—法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
 德國工業生產在 7 月月減 1.2%後，8 月回彈 2%，主要反映部分汽車業者在 7
月提前進行歲修，並且在 8 月陸續復工後，帶動生產及庫存回補需求所致；此外，
受到 8 月萊茵河水位降低，沿岸化學工廠物流運輸受阻，致化工產業產出月減
1.2%為主要拖累因素。儘管工業生產如期反彈，惟工業訂單卻大幅下跌 10.6%，
主要是與軍工產業相關的訂單如飛機、船舶、軍用車輛、武器彈藥等訂單減少，
顯示政府前期大額採購效應逐漸消退；除軍工產業外，其餘產業訂單則大致維持
穩定。 
 法國 8 月工業生產月減 0.3%，為連續第二個月下滑，主要係夏季用電高峰過
後電力產出回落，帶動能源部門生產減少所致。另方面，製造業生產月增 0.3%，
其中造紙、化工及電子電機設備等產業自前期低點回升，部分抵銷汽車等運輸設
備產出月減 2.2%的拖累。不過，法國 9 月 INSEE 新訂單指標由 8 月的-13.8 點降
至-15.8 點，顯示企業接單偏弱，主要反映出口需求疲軟。此外，相較於德國近期
透過國防及基礎建設支出擴大支撐工業生產，法國財政擴張力道相對有限，使製
造業景氣較依賴外部需求，德、法兩國製造業表現因而呈現逐漸分化的趨勢。 
 展望未來，近期歐元區各國殖利率上升，除反映通膨壓力增強、升息預增加
之外，亦部分反映西班牙宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選、法國財政前景不明朗
等政治因素影響。儘管較高的殖利率有助於促使法國政府推動財政整頓，但考量
相關改革涉及福利及退休制度調整等政治敏感議題，不確定性較高，短期內德國
與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。整體而言，法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐
相對有限下，景氣復甦力道恐仍較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現
分化的情形短期內仍將持續。 
三、日本—薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
 8 月薪資年增 3.8%，其中經常性薪資年增 3.8%、加班費年增 5.2%、實質薪資
年增 1.5%，主要受惠於春鬥加薪效應延續及通膨壓力較去年趨緩所帶動，有助於
進一步提升民眾購買力，支撐消費與經濟復甦。

四、中國—歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
中國 9 月外匯存底降至 3.40 兆美元，較前月減少 381 億美元，反映美元升
值、政府以外匯購買黃金儲備，加上歐美殖利率上升，造成中國持有的海外債券
資產出現評價損失，皆為外匯存底下滑的主要原因。惟亦不排除中國外匯流入情
況可能有所轉弱，例如出口成長放緩、進口需求增加，或資本外流規模高於預期
等因素。 
展望未來，近期歐中談判達成 16 項共識，未來中國混合動力車出口至歐盟將
面臨調整壓力，但歐盟進一步升高貿易保護措施的風險已降溫。另一方面，基於
中國維持大額經常帳順差、出口商結匯需求及年底季節性資金流入支撐，預期人
民幣仍有升值空間，並帶動外匯存底回升。 
五、印度及東南亞—世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息 
(1)世界銀行 2026 年 10 月《東亞與太平洋地區經濟展望報告》指出，受人工智慧
（AI）相關出口與投資強勁帶動，將 2026 年東亞與太平洋地區（EAP）經濟成長
率預測上修 0.3 個百分點至 4.5%，2027 年與 2028 年將分別放緩至 4.4%與 4.3%，
其中，越南、馬來西亞、泰國 GDP 大幅上調至 7.4%、5.1%及 2.0%。世銀指出，
AI 相關商品占多數經濟體出口增長的一半以上，其中，馬來西亞、菲律賓、泰國
和越南的 AI 出口貢獻率更超過七成，但扣除 AI 相關產品後，區域貿易成長呈現
疲弱或負增長，警告該地區的經濟韌性高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容
易受到全球科技支出逆轉的影響，建議應將 AI 產品生產優勢轉化為廣泛應用，
以提升生產力，並創造更多優質就業機會。此外，美伊戰爭推升能源價格，各國
政府比其他地區更積極因應，也更仰賴補貼，此將增加財政成本並減少外匯存底，
世銀預估中東石油出口要到 2027 年中才會恢復至戰前水準，能源成本上升已對
製造業造成壓力，運輸價格大漲也已打擊消費需求，持續性的補貼可能難以為繼。

(2)印度:印度央行 10/7 貨幣政策會議宣布升息 1 碼至 5.5%，為近四年來首度升

方面，印度內需、投資及基礎建設支出持續支撐景氣，央行將 2026/27 財年經濟

攀升及氣候因素影響，將 2026/27 財年 CPI 通膨率預測上調至 5.2%，高於 4%的
中期目標。另外，世界銀行 10 月最新《南亞經濟展望》報告，上修印度 2026/27
財年經濟成長率 0.4 個百分點至 7.1%，反映內需消費、製造業、服務業及出口表
現優於預期，然而，中東衝突導致能源價格上漲、聖嬰現象衝擊農業生產、全球
金融市場波動及資本流動風險，仍可能影響經濟前景。整體而言，印度經濟成長
動能仍穩健，但外部風險及輸入性通膨壓力增加，未來貨幣政策將以維持物價穩
定為優先，並有進一步升息的可能。 
 (3)越南: 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率達 9.95%，高於上季修正後的 8.81%，並
創 4 年新高，累計前三季經濟成長率達 9.01%。成長動能主要來自製造業與營建
業擴張、外人直接投資持續流入、以及外貿大幅成長。各產業均穩健成長，工業
及建築業成長 12.50%(占 GDP 總值 51.57%)；服務業成長 9.54%(占 43.29%)；農

21.39%，貨物和服務出、進口分別成長 23.27%、28.75%。展望未來，越南受惠於
全球企業分散供應鏈、AI 產業需求及政府推動基礎建設與數位轉型，經濟前景仍
偏正向。

六、台灣—出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.73%，創近兩年半新高，已連續 5 個
月高於 2%警戒線，主要係受豪雨影響，蔬果供應減少帶動價格上漲，加上汽機
車減稅效應淡化、中秋節落點不同及國際油價維持高檔，共同推升物價。除蔬果
外，外食費、房租、電費及交通相關費用皆持續上漲，顯示物價壓力仍在，但主
計總處認為整體漲勢仍屬溫和，預估 10 月 CPI 將略為回落至約 2.5%。 
9 月出口額達 872.2 億美元，年增 60.9%，其中資通產品出口倍增、電子零組
件年增 45.3%。財政部指出，在 AI 相關投資持續、部分電子產品供應偏緊、新品
上市與旺季拉貨等因素共同挹注下，出口表現明顯升溫；隨著 AI 應用擴大及年
底採購需求增加，全年出口額有望突破 9,000 億美元。 
美國國際貿易委員會（USITC）於 10 月 9 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 337 條
款，對特定垂直供電系統及相關產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達及鴻
海旗下相關企業等遭列名。若 337 條款調查最終認定侵權，相關產品可能遭禁止
輸入美國，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。

1 事件：10月外贸数据公布
2026年 10月，我国货物贸易（下同）进出口总值 4.87 万亿
元，增长 31.2%。其中，出口 2.79 万亿元，增长 28.7%；进口
2.08万亿元，增长 34.7%；贸易顺差 7127.76亿元。按美元计价，
10月我国进出口总值 7193.83亿美元，增长 37.9%。其中，出口
4123.3亿美元，增长 35.2%；进口 3070.53亿美元，增长 41.6%；
贸易顺差 1052.77亿美元。
2 事件解析：数据特征和变动原因
2.1 进出口维持高增
10 月进出口增速（美元计价，下同）维持高增，顺差仍在高
位。出口增速二季度以来维持强势，10 月高位小幅下降。从主要
出口市场表现来看，对美出口有所回落，增速仍在 30%以上，对
日本、东盟、欧盟出口增速均有所下降，对东盟的出口增速高达
40%以上。金砖四国方面，除巴西外均有所下降，但整体增速均
可观。从主要出口商品增速来看，除了通用设备、自动化数据处
理设备以及集成电路外均有所下滑，AI 相关产品出口仍维持高景

气。
进口增速同样有所加快，增速突破 40%。从进口数量来看，
主要进口商品增速除大豆、钢材以外均有所回升；从进口金额来
看，原油、煤、铜、二极管、集成电路有所回升，其中自动化数
据处理设备虽有所下降，但增速高达 261%。AI 相关产品的进口
需求较为强劲。

3 事件影响：对经济和市场
3.1 AI 相关需求旺盛，进出口维持高增
10 月进出口增速维持高增。出口增速二季度以来维持强势，
10月高位小幅下降。从主要出口市场表现来看，对美出口有所回
落，增速仍在 30%以上，对日本、东盟、欧盟出口增速均有所下

AI 相关产品的进口需求较为强劲。本月顺差维持在高位，但由于
进口增长更快，顺差高位回落。
4 事件预测：趋势判断
4.1 促进贸易平衡发展
我国外贸在此前关税扰动下表现韧性，我们认为主要还是中
国制造业在世界经济格局中的优势地位所决定的，作为全球制造

中心，这也是我国应对外部不确定性的优势，并且也得到了进一
步的加强巩固。我们认为外部环境的改善，中美关税继续暂停以

宏观数据

及全球 AI 产业链的发展，也推动了贸易的加速增长。政策方面，
根据 7 月政治局会议的安排，要拓展国际经贸互利合作空间，大
力发展服务贸易，促进贸易平衡发展；实施好更加积极的财政政
策和适度宽松的货币政策，充分发挥各项存量政策效能，及时谋
划出台务实管用的增量政策，加大逆周期调节力度，推动经济持
续向新向优向好发展，努力实现“十五五”良好开局。

聯強(2347)：資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢
不變 
建議：維持買進

一、聯強為亞太地區最大 3C 通
路商，營收成長以半導體業務最
佳  
 聯強為 3C 電子產品之銷售、配送、維修、量身訂做即時生產之專業通路商，為
亞太地區 3C 通路商龍頭，2026 年前三季產品比重分別為商用加值佔 31%、資
訊消費佔 17% 、 半 導 體 佔 48% 、 手 機 產 品 佔 4% ，成長主要來商用加值
(+112%YoY)和半導體業務(+162%YoY)。聯強海外市場包含中國、香港、紐澳、
泰國、印尼、印度及越南等地，其中以中國、台灣和印尼等區域較佳，營收分別
為 YoY+109%、YoY+78%和 YoY+18%。聯強競爭優勢為透過其四合一經營模
式，搭配其綿密的通路發揮規模經濟，並提供高附加價值的後勤運籌(庫存管理
與快速維為修服務等)，於中國主要競爭對手為神州數碼、天音通信控股。

二、半導體業務成長性佳，3Q26 
EPS 2.93 元創新高並優於預期 
 3Q26 營收 2366 億元(+18%QoQ；+136%YoY)優於預估值 14%，其中商用加
值和半導體營收分別為+116%YOY 和+273%YoY，因秉棄部分利潤不佳專案，
毛利率 3.49%優於前季之 3.4%，營業利益 61.41 億元(+28%%QoQ)優於預估
值 24%，因營運資金需求以及籌資成本上升致使利息費用提高至 9.34 億元
(+106%QoQ)，稅後純益 48.83 億元(+25%QoQ；+90%YoY)，EPS 2.93 元優
於預期之 2.5 元並創新高。

三、資金成本上揚導致業務拓展
較為謹慎，估計 4Q26 EPS 2.34
元 
 由於資金成本上揚致使公司放棄部分利潤不佳半導體業務，十月營收月減 34%，
估計 4Q26 營收 2046 億元(-14%QoQ；YoY+62%)，因產品組合變化轉佳，毛
利率提升至 3.58%，稅後純益 38.99 億元(-20%QoQ；+41%YoY)，EPS 2.34
元。

四、AI 長線需求有助商用加值和
半導體業務成長性 
 集邦 預估 2026 和 2027 年全 球九 大 CSP 資 本支 出分 別為 0.95 兆 美元
(+106%YoY)和 1.53 兆美元(+60%YoY)，且 AI 需求從雲端擴展到企業端，其中
以商用加值和半導體業務受惠幅度最大。 
在商用加值業務方面，在台灣和中國資料中心產品出貨挹注下，3Q26 營收
+116%YoY。展望 2027 年，中國十十五規劃和國產化政策推動政府和國企加速
採用本土品牌，推升聯強代理華為、浪潮等品牌業務成長性，台灣則是接獲數個
半導體大廠 AI 基礎建設專案，加計中小企業 AI 建置需求逐步顯現，商用業務將
為營運成長主要動能之一。 
在半導體業務方面，缺貨和漲價效致使記憶體、SSD 和硬碟業務為半導體業務
中 成 長 性 最 佳 者 ， 且 AI 推 論 亦 增 加 CPU 需求，3Q26 半 導 體 業 務 營 收

公司拜訪快報 
YoY+273%，半導體業務為營運成長另一重點業務，但因資金成本上揚，公司將
摒棄部分利潤未達目標專案，此將影響短期營收動能。 
受惠於出貨 ASP 增加，以及原廠擴大促銷力道挽救銷售量，3Q26 資訊家用和
手機業務分別+21%YoY 和+33%YoY。記憶體大漲致使原廠逐步反應成本於 PC
和手機新機售價，並將導致消費者延長換機週期，2027 年此兩塊業務成長性仍
相對保守看待。

五、資金成本上揚將致使接案更
為謹慎，但長期營益率好轉方向
不變 
 聯強今年接獲數個大型 AI 基礎建設專案，致使營收動能以及營運資金需求大增，
3Q26 遇到資金成本上揚，致使利息費用較前季倍增，公司將透過聯貸、可轉債
以及現增降低利息負擔，預期三項籌資工具皆完成後可望降低利息費用 20-
30%。展望 2027 年，儘管營收成長性不如 2026 年亮眼，但接單策略轉趨謹慎
將致使毛利率下滑壓力降低，且預期營業費用增幅有限下，營益率持續好轉方向
不變。

六、維持買進理由  1)預估 2026 和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 9.38 元和 11.07 元。 
2)儘管資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢不變，維持買進
評等，TP 100 元 (2027 PE x 9)。

### Page 1

ab 10 November 2026
Global Research
First Read
Inventec
AI Servers into a transition quarter ahead of
2027 ramps
Q326 mixed as GPU server ramps dampened margins
Inventec hosted its Q3 results Tues with sales NT$272.1bn, +1% QoQ broadly in-line
with UBSe/street flat/-2%.   Servers surpassed PCs to reach 51% of Q3 sales, with more
than 50% from rack-level AI GPU servers (B300, HGX, NVL72) and ASIC L6 boards (TPU,
Axion, Graviton), while general servers were constrained by components. Notebook

shipments were 4.9mn in Q3, -9% QoQ/YoY with revenue -5% QoQ to 47% of sales,
while smart devices remained 2%. Mix shift to lower margin GPU servers, higher pass-
through component costs from inflation and overseas facility ramp costs dampened
GM/OpM to 4.2%/1.6% below Street 4.6%/2.0%, dampening EPS to NT$0.71, below
UBSe/Street NT$0.76/NT$0.94. Inventory grew NT$32bn to NT$162bn QoQ, lifting days
from 46 to 57 as end customers require more buffer stock.

Q4 a transition quarter for AI GPU ramps
We lower Q4 from flat to -9% QoQ and below prior street +1% QoQ as GPU servers
slow ahead of the VR200 ramp ramping more in Q127 and notebooks drop a further
low-mid-teens. General server mix is improving on healthy demand from CSP customers
securing better priority component allocation.  The positive mix should improve GM/
OpM to +30bp/+10bp to 4.5%/1.7%.  For the full year, server units are growing 25-30%

YoY and revenue 55% YoY while notebook units are now trending down 6% YoY
although higher ASPs passing on memory are lifting sales +35% YoY.
2027 growth drivers stay solid
We lift 2027E sales from +25% YoY to +29% YoY, above prior street +23% YoY with
growth from general server ramps from its two ASIC CPU programs, ramp of L6 boards
for AMD Helios from Q426, and R200 NVIDIA orders ramping up at higher ASPs
alongside VR200 racks to a top 5 CSP. We keep 2027E GMs/OpMs fairly stable at

4.5%/1.9% with component costs staying elevated with AI capex already projected up
sharply again next year and keeping the supply chain tight. For 2028, we have some
moderation to +20% YoY growth to allow for some moderation to capex from power
availability and funding.  For Inventec, capex is set to lower from US$1bn in 2026 to US
$500mn in 2027, allowing mgmt to fund with debt over additional equity.
Valuation: Maintain Neutral and trim our PT
We reflect component inflation and trim 2026-27E reported EPS from NT$3.50/NT$4.25

to NT$3.40/NT$4.15. We trim our PT from NT$65 to NT$59, also lowering from 15x
2027E to 14x, +1 STD Deviation above its 12x average as it increases its server mix over
low margin NBs. We maintain Neutral balancing growth potentially above street on
leverage to AI, ASIC and general servers but offset by muted margins and valuation
already at the high-end of its peer group at 13x 2027 vs. group trading at 9-13x P/E.

Company Description
Founded in 1975, Inventec Corporation is an electronics contract manufacturer supporting
customers' engineering design, product development, assembly and distribution. It is
primarily engaged in the design and manufacturing of servers, personal computers and
mobile devices. In 1998, it acquired Digital Equipment Corporation's Taiwan assets to expand

its server R&D and manufacturing capabilities, allowing it to become a global top-three server
supplier. Inventec is also a global top-five notebook PC manufacturer. During 9M20, PC
accounted for 52% of its sales; servers 39%; mobile devices 8%; and others 1%.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's
interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of

shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Inventec based on PE multiple methodology. Downside risks for Inventec include: 1)
a lower-than-expected notebook market share recovery; 2) weaker-than-expected growth in
server markets; and 3) unfavourable FX movement. Upside risks for Inventec include: 1) a
higher-than-expected notebook market share recovery; 2) stronger-than-expected growth in

server markets; and 3) favourable FX movement.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

### Page 1

Summary: Merida posted its 3Q26 results on 11 November after market hours. Merida’s 
gross margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our 
estimate (16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%). Its 3Q26 EPS was TWD1.06, lower than our 
forecast (TWD1.68) and the consensus estimate (TWD1.65). Merida plans to hold an 
investor call on 17 November and we will provide an update then.

We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. For more information on this company, please 
refer to our latest flash note, 2Q26 review: mainly dragged by Specialized, on 14 August.

What’s the impact 
• 3Q26 results missed. Merida’s 3Q26 revenue of TWD6,098m (-12.1% QoQ, -15.6% 
YoY) missed our and the Bloomberg consensus estimates by 13.6% and 11.1%, 
respectively, which we attributed to the discount when destocking the older models. Its 
gross margin came in at 15.2% (vs. 19.8% in 2Q26; 13.7% in 3Q25), lower than our 
estimate (16.2%) and the consensus (17.3%), due to less OBM sales and the price 
discounting for further inventory digestion. Its operating profit of TWD384m (-49.8% 
QoQ, -9.7% YoY) also missed our and the consensus estimates by 35.2% and 37.3%, 
respectively. Its operating margin came in at 6.3% (vs. 11.0% in 2Q26; 5.9% in 3Q25), 
missing our estimate of 8.4% and the consensus of 8.9%. Considering the equity 
investment loss of TWD150m+, its net profit of TWD176m (-44.8% QoQ, -71.9% YoY) 
missed our and the consensus by 65.0% and 65.6%. Its 3Q26 EPS was TWD1.06, lower 
than our forecast (TWD1.68) and the consensus estimate (TWD1.65).

Merida: 3Q26 results vs. Daiwa and the Bloomberg estimates 
(TWDm)  Merida 3Q26 results   Daiwa forecast  Difference  Bloomberg consensus Difference

Source: Company, Bloomberg, Daiwa forecasts

• 4Q26 outlook. We forecast its 4Q26 revenue to decline QoQ as the company will 
continue to manage its existing models and expects new models in 2027 to bring 
stronger demand.

What we recommend 
We have a Buy (1) rating with a 12-month TP of TWD96, based on a target PER of 17x 
applied on our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. The stock is currently trading at PERs of 
13.3x/10.7x, based on our 2026/27E EPS, vs. its past-5-year one-year-forward PER trading 
range of 7-33x. Key downside risks: slower-than-expected e-bike penetration in the EU and 
the US, worse-than-expected demand in an inflationary environment and inventory digestion.

In the interests of timeliness, this document has not been edited.

Lotes Co. Ltd  | Asia Pacific
3Q GM Decline Eases; 4Q
Recovery in Focus
3Q results:
• GM came in at 44.8% (-60bps q/q, -640bps y/y), 20bps above our estimate.
3Q GM continued to face margin headwinds from rising raw material costs,
which we highlighted in our recent upgrade report here. We look forward to
management's comment during its results call to see if 4Q margin will be
guided to return to growth.
• Opex was  higher than expected at NT$2.2bn, 10% above our estimate and

9% above the Street.
• So OpM wa only 22.9% (+100bps q/q, -700bps y/y), 180bps below our
estimate and 250bps below the Street.
• Net profit was NT$1,919mn (-3% q/q, -17% y/y) for EPS of NT$17.09, 12%
below our estimate and 12% below the Street.
• Lotes is holding an earnings call on November 13 at 4 PM HKT; we will
provide takeaways after the call.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Lotes Co. Ltd. (3533.TW)
Primary - Residual Income
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity of 9.6% (beta of
1.0, equity risk premium of 8.7% and risk-free rate of 1.0%), a medium-term growth rate of
12.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected growth in the server business, such as from cloud and enter-
prise in China

n Stronger-than-expected DT demand
n Sooner-than-expected inventory digestion of NB
n Faster ramp-up of its EV business
Risks to Downside
n Further deceleration of server demand from US hyperscalers
n Weaker-than-expected DT demand
n Slower-than-expected inventory digestion of NB
n More severe pricing erosion driven by competition

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

漲 停 ， 本 益 比 已 達 43 倍 ， 加 上 新 產 線 預 計 2029 年才開始第一期量產， 較 市 場 預 期
2027~2028 年量產晚， 短 期 獲 利 貢 獻 有 限 ， 且 仍 有 設 備 資 本 支 出 、 折 舊 及 CB 潛在稀
釋 風 險 ， 因 此 短 線 不 宜 追 價 。

研 究 員 ： 陳 俐 妍   
ly.c@capital.com.tw

台 積 電 擴 大 先 進 封 裝 外 包 布 局 ， 精 材 預 計 2029 年切入 CoWoS 後段 oS 製
程 ：  
精材(3374 TT) 於 11/13/2026 公 告 董 事 會 決 議 通 過 與 具 重 大 影 響 之 投 資 者 台
積電(2330 TT) 簽 訂 產 能 外 包 協 議 ， 契 約 期 間 自 2026/11/13 至 2033/12/31，
雙 方 將 針 對 先 進 封 裝 後 段 製 程 展 開 長 期 策 略 合 作。根 據 公 告 內 容，精 材 計 畫
進駐台積電中部科學園區廠房建置產線並負責生產營運，由台積電提供廠
房空間及先進封裝技術與製造能力支援，精材則自行投入生產設備及自動
物 料 搬 運 系 統 (AMHS)資 本 支 出，切 入 先 進 封 裝 供 應 鏈 中 的 oS(on-Substrate)
模 組 。 該 產 線 規 劃 於 2029 年 實 現 第 一 期 量 產 ， 雙 方 並 將 共 同 確 保 新 產 線 的
技 術 規 範、品 質 及 良 率 符 合 台 積 電 與 客 戶 要 求。此 外，精 材 董 事 會 同 步 通 過
發 行 新 台 幣 150 億 元 可 轉 換 公 司 債 (CB)， 以 支 應 初 期 產 能 建 置 需 求 。  
既 有 台 積 電 合 作 關 係 延 伸 至 先 進 封 裝 ， 有 助 精 材 拓 展 AI 及 HPC 業務：  
就 精 材 本 身 而 言，此 次 合 作 是 既 有 台 積 電 供 應 鏈 關 係 的 深 化。精 材 原 以 CIS
封 裝 及 晶 圓 級 封 裝 (WLCSP) 為 主 要 業 務，並 承 接 台 積 電 晶 圓 測 試 (CP)外 包 訂
單 ， 惟 既 有 測 試 機 台 於 08/2026 已 達 滿 載 ， 且 受 限 於 無 新 增 無 塵 室 空 間 ， 部
分 台 積 電 測 試 訂 單 轉 由 欣 銓 (3264 TT)承 接。此 次 透 過 進 駐 台 積 電 中 科 廠 區，
精材得以突破自身廠房空間限制，免除購地建廠所需的前置時間與土地成
本，並 利 用 台 積 電 既 有 廠 區 及 技 術 支 援，降 低 新 產 線 建 置 與 製 程 導 入 的 部 分
門 檻。由 於 台 積 電 目 前 持 有 精 材 約 41%股 權，雙 方 已 有 長 期 合 作 基 礎，此 次
進 一 步 由 晶 圓 測 試 及 既 有 封 裝 業 務 延 伸 至 CoWoS 後段 oS 製 程 ， 有 助 精 材
擴 大 與 台 積 電 的 合 作 範 圍，並 提 高 未 來 AI 及 HPC 相 關 業 務 的 營 收 占 比。不
過，CP 測 試 與 oS 屬 不 同 製 程，仍 須 觀 察 新 產 線 設 備 裝 機、製 程 驗 證 及 良 率
爬 升 進 度 。  
台 積 電 擴 大 先 進 封 裝 供 應 鏈 分 工 ， Interposer 與 oS 同 步 建 立 外 部 產 能 ：  
此 次 精 材 切 入 CoWoS 後段 oS 製 程 ， 應 放 在 台 積 電 持 續 擴 大 先 進 封 裝 供 應
鏈 分 工 的 整 體 策 略 下 觀 察 。 隨 AI 與 HPC 晶片朝 Chiplet、 HBM 及 異 質 整 合
發展，CoWoS 不 同 製 程 環 節 均 面 臨 產 能 擴 充 需 求 。 台 積 電 除 持 續 擴 充 自 身
先 進 封 裝 產 能 外，亦 逐 步 透 過 外 部 合 作 夥 伴 增 加 不 同 環 節 的 供 應 能 力，例 如
Interposer 方 面 與 GlobalFoundries 合 作 建 立 外 部 供 應 來 源，並 透 過 世 界 先 進
等 業 者 擴 充 相 關 產 能；後 段 先 進 封 裝 則 持 續 擴 大 與 OSAT 的 合 作，此 次 再 由
持股約 41%的 精 材 規 劃 於 台 積 電 中 科 廠 區 建 置 oS 產 線 ， 顯 示 台 積 電 正 逐 步
建 立 結 合 關 係 企 業 與 外 部 供 應 商 的 先 進 封 裝 供 應 鏈，以 提 高 產 能 配 置 彈 性。 
不 同 製 程 環 節 的 外 包 條 件 與 策 略 並 不 相 同 。 Interposer 外 包 主 要 在 於 增 加 供
應 來 源 及 產 能 彈 性；CoWoS 則 可 區 分 為 CoW 與 oS 兩 個 階 段，其 中 CoW 涉
及 晶 圓、矽 中 介 層 及 晶 片 間 的 高 精 度 整 合，製 程 複 雜 度 與 良 率 要 求 較 高，外

### Page 2

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 11 月 16 日

2 群益投顧 
包 仍 受 技 術 及 良 率 條 件 限 制；相 較 之 下，oS 主 要 負 責 將 完 成 的 CoW 模 組 接
合 至 基 板，隨 OSAT 業 者 製 程 經 驗 累 積 及 良 率 逐 步 成 熟，已 成 為 較 適 合 擴 大
委 外 的 環 節 ， 目 前 台 積 電 CoWoS 後段 oS 製 程 已 大 量 採 用 外 包 模 式 。 由 於
OSAT 的 先 進 封 裝 毛 利 率 通 常 低 於 台 積 電 整 體 製 造 業 務 ， 將 部 分 oS 產 能 交
由 外 部 業 者 承 接，亦 有 助 台 積 電 將 有 限 的 無 塵 室 空 間、設 備 及 工 程 資 源 優 先
配 置 於 技 術 門 檻 較 高 的 CoW 及 次 世 代 先 進 封 裝 製 程 。  
精材 2029 年 才 量 產 ， 2026～ 2028 年 既 有 OSAT 仍 持 續 受 惠 AI 封 裝 需 求 ：  
精 材 新 產 線 預 計 2029 年 才 開 始 第 一 期 量 產，因 此 在 2026～ 2028 年 間，並 不
會立即改變既有封測業者的供需格局。台積電雖可依製程整合需求向客戶
建 議 合 作 封 測 廠，但 客 戶 仍 可 依 自 身 需 求 直 接 與 封 測 業 者 洽 談 產 能 及 合 作，
因 此 先 進 封 裝 訂 單 並 非 完 全 由 台 積 電 單 方 面 分 配。在 AI/HPC 晶 片 持 續 推 動
先 進 封 裝 規 格 升 級 下，日 月 光 投 控、力 成 及 其 他 既 有 封 測 業 者 仍 可 持 續 受 惠
於新增外包需求，並透過製程改善、良率提升及設備效率優化增加有效產
出。 
至 2029 年 後 ， 精 材 oS 新 產 線 及 其 他 業 者 新 增 產 能 將 陸 續 開 出 ， 加 上 既 有
OSAT 持 續 透 過 良 率 改 善 增 加 有 效 產 能 ， 供 應 鏈 競 爭 可 能 逐 步 加 劇 。 因 此 ，
中 長 期 仍 須 觀 察 AI/HPC 需 求 成 長 能 否 持 續 吸 收 新 增 設 備 及 良 率 提 升 所 帶
來的有效供給，這將是影響先進封裝產業供需及各業者獲利表現的重要變
數。 
2029 年 後 新 增 有 效 產 能 可 能 增 加 ， 精 材 仍 面 臨 資 本 支 出 與 股 本 稀 釋 壓 力 ：  
雖然 AI/HPC 晶 片 持 續 朝 先 進 製 程、Chiplet 及 異 質 整 合 發 展，將 支 撐 先 進 封
裝 長 期 需 求 ， 但 目 前 CoWoS 供 不 應 求 ， 除 AI/HPC 需 求 快 速 成 長 、 晶 片 封
裝 面 積 擴 大 及 HBM 整 合 增 加 外 ， 製 程 複 雜 度 高 、 良 率 尚 待 提 升 ， 亦 可 能 限
制 有 效 產 能 釋 出。值 得 留 意 的 是，隨 製 程 技 術 持 續 改 進、設 備 調 校 及 量 產 經
驗 累 積，既 有 產 線 良 率 若 逐 步 提 升，即 使 未 新 增 設 備，也 可 能 增 加 有 效 產 出；
若 再 加 上 台 積 電 及 全 球 OSAT 業 者 持 續 擴 充 產 能 ， 未 來 供 給 增 幅 可 能 高 於
市 場 目 前 預 期 。 因 此 ， 現 階 段 CoWoS 供 不 應 求 未 必 能 長 期 延 續 ， 若 良 率 瓶
頸 逐 步 突 破，供 需 可 能 由 緊 俏 轉 向 平 衡，甚 至 出 現 供 給 較 為 充 裕 的 情 況，後
續 仍 須 觀 察 AI/HPC 需 求 成 長 能 否 吸 收 新 增 設 備 及 良 率 改 善 所 帶 來 的 有 效
供給。  
此 外 ， 精 材 須 自 行 負 擔 設 備 及 AMHS 資 本 支 出 ， 設 備 投 資 至 新 產 線 營 收 貢
獻 仍 有 時 間 落 差，設 備 陸 續 投 入 使 用 後 亦 將 增 加 折 舊 費 用，若 初 期 產 能 利 用
率 及 良 率 爬 升 不 如 預 期，可 能 對 毛 利 率 及 營 業 利 益 形 成 壓 力。資 金 方 面，精
材截至 11/2026 股 本 為 27.14 億 元 ， 約 當 2.714 億 股 ， 預 計 發 行 150 億元
CB， 目 前 尚 未 確 定 轉 換 價 格 ； 若 以 11/13/2026 收 盤 價 519 元 作 為 假 設 轉 換
價格，並以全數轉換估算，新增股數約 2,890 萬 股 ， 相 較 目 前 股 數 增 加 約
10.65%， 轉 換 後 原 股 東 持 股 比 例 將 稀 釋 約 9.63%。 惟 此 為 假 設 情 境 ， 實 際 稀
釋 程 度 仍 須 視 最 終 發 行 金 額、轉 換 價 格 及 轉 換 條 件 而 定。因 此，此 次 合 作 雖
有助精材長期業務結構升級，但仍須考量資本支出回收效益、折舊吸收能
力、CB 潛 在 稀 釋 及 未 來 產 業 供 需 變 化 ， 不 宜 直 接 以 目 前 CoWoS 供 不 應 求
的 狀 況 外 推 未 來 獲 利 。

3 群益投顧 
精 材 短 線 評 價 已 反 映 部 分 成 長 預 期 ， 封 測 股 則 可 留 意 修 正 後 的 布 局 機 會 ：  
精 材 此 次 取 得 台 積 電 CoWoS 後段 oS 產 能 外 包 合 作 ， 對 長 期 業 務 結 構 升 級
具 正 面 意 義 ， 惟 股 價 於 公 告 前 已 連 續 6 個 交 易 日 上 漲 ， 累 計 漲 幅 約 40%，
11/13/2026 收 盤 達 519 元 漲 停，本 益 比 已 達 43 倍，加 上 新 產 線 預 計 2029 年
才 開 始 第 一 期 量 產 ， 短 期 獲 利 貢 獻 有 限 ， 且 仍 有 設 備 資 本 支 出 、 折 舊 及 CB
潛 在 稀 釋 風 險 ， 因 此 短 線 不 宜 追 價 。  
另一方面，不宜因精材取得台積電合作，就立即下修日月光投控(3711 TT)
及力成(6239 TT) 的 中 短 期 投 資 看 法 。 2026 年至 2028 年 精 材 新 產 線 尚 未 量
產，既 有 封 測 廠 仍 可 持 續 承 接 AI 及 HPC 先 進 封 裝 與 測 試 外 溢 訂 單，並 透 過
產 線 效 率 及 良 率 改 善 提 升 有 效 產 出，中 短 期 應 以 訂 單 能 見 度、產 能 利 用 率 及
獲 利 表 現 作 為 主 要 觀 察 重 點。4Q26 以 來，部 分 封 測 股 已 因 市 場 對 AI 資 本 支
出 及 投 資 效 益 的 疑 慮 而 大 幅 修 正，若 後 續 訂 單 及 營 運 展 望 未 明 顯 轉 弱，評 價
修正反而可能提供布局機會，可持續留意受惠先進封裝需求的日月光投控
(3711 TT) 、力成(6239 TT)， 以 及 承 接 晶 圓 測 試 外 溢 需 求 的 欣 銓 (3264 TT)。
至 2029 年 後 ， 隨 精 材 及 其 他 業 者 新 增 產 能 陸 續 開 出 ， 加 上 既 有 產 線 良 率 提
升 帶 動 有 效 供 給 增 加 ， 則 須 留 意 供 需 變 化 及 競 爭 加 劇 風 險 。

### Page 4

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 11 月 16 日

4 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】  
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 ≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <15%

中立(Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等  
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整  
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】  
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較 3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為 四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等 係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。   
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」 及 「 區 間 操 作 」 均有 upside 目 標 價 。 差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。  
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ：  
1. 達 目 標 價 。  
2. 雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。  
3. 推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】 
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

기아 (000270)

Company Brief 
자동차/모빌리티 
Analyst 김준성 
joonsung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY)으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY)을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 150,000원 
현재주가 (10.30) 95,600원 
상승여력 56.9%

삼성전자 
(005930) 
 결국 제자리를 찾아갈 것 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 500,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
231,000 
반도체업종

◼ 3분기 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 
◼ 주주환원은 시점의 문제일 뿐. 강화 방향성은 여전히 유효. 
◼ 메모리반도체 이익의 폭증이 가격의 매력을 유발. 비중확대 전략 유효.

매력적인 가격, 변하지 않은 주주환원 정책의 방향성 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 메모리반도체 주도 폭발적 이익 상승이 가격 (주
가)의 매력을 지속 유발하고 있고, 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향성에도 변한 것
은 없다는 판단. 절대적으로 싼 주가를 비중 확대의 기회로 삼을 필요. 
1. 주주환원, 시점의 문제 
금번 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 대외 불확실성이 큰 만큼, 
시장은 자사주 매입/소각과 같은 즉각적 주가 부양 정책의 발표를 기대.  
당사는 삼성전자의 주주환원이 “시점의 문제”라 생각. 총 주주환원의 규모도, 
차기 (2027-2029년) 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향에도 변한 것은 없다는 판
단. 시장이 기대하는 자사주 매입/소각은 우선주 중심으로 시행될 가능성이 
높으며, 시행 시, 그 규모는 25조원 내외일 것 (DPS 12,567원)으로 전망. 
2. 메모리반도체, 더 올라갈 것 
보수적 환율 전망 (2027년 평균 환율 1,300원 적용)에도, 2027년 메모리반도
체 영업이익은 608조원에 달할 것. 이익 전망의 상향 여력 또한 유효. 
1) 최대 생산, 최대 판매 
삼성전자는 금일 4Q26 DRAM 판매량 가이던스로 전분기 대비 10% 중후반 
성장 (q-q +18%로 추정)을 제시. 일부 투자자들은 높아진 기저 부담으로 
2027년 판매 성장이 제한될 것을 우려. 당사는 높아진 기저에도 2027년 
DRAM 판매가 전년대비 20% 상승할 것으로 전망. 
- 극단적 수급 불균형 속, 삼성전자는 “최대 생산, 최대 판매” 정책을 시행 
중. 삼성전자만의 강력한 Execution이 20% 대 성장을 유지시킬 원동력. 
- DRAM 1c 수율의 조기 개선 속, 범용 DRAM 양산에 DRAM 1c Capa가 일
부 투입될 것으로 예상. Bit 성장을 높일 수 있는 변화가 될 것. 
2) 열려 있는 가격의 상방 
1Q27 모바일 DRAM에서 Surprise가 발생할 것이라는 의견을 유지 (전분기 
대비 30% 이상 상승). 장기계약이 체결된 CSP향으로도 가격의 추가 상승 여
력이 존재한다는 점을 상기할 필요. CSP의 경우, 당장의 비용 부담보다 AI 시
장 선점을 우선시. 장기계약으로 형성된 가격 Band의 천장 (Ceiling) 또한 시
장 우려 대비 낮지 않음을 감안할 필요. 2027년 범용 DRAM ASP는 전년대비 
40% 중후반까지 상승할 가능성 (현 전망: 전년대비 34% 상승).  
3) HBM, 제품 경쟁력과 점유율의 동반 성장  
최근 삼성전자향으로 HBM4/HBM4e 추가 공급 요청이 쇄도하고 있는 것으
로 추정. 일부 고객은 연내 추가 공급 시 2027년 계약 가격을 선제 지급할 
의사를 표출. 제품 경쟁력이 회복되었음을 보여주는 증거라 생각. 
제품 경쟁력의 회복과 최대 Capa의 이점 속, 2027년은 HBM 점유율 1위를 
확보하는 기념비적 해가 될 것으로 전망 (매출액 기준 점유율 38% 예상).

KOSPI 5937.66 
시가총액 1,503,893십억원

2 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Inflation Sticky but Overheating Less Clear
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The disconnect between very strong recent growth with stubborn inflation above the Fed’s 2%
target on one hand and more moderate business surveys and labor-market indicators on the
other, has complicated the optimal Fed policy path and contributed to large swings in interest

rates. Indeed, the economy looks strong in several respects. Real GDP grew at a 3.4% annualized
pace in Q3, led by 3.8% real consumer spending growth, according to Bureau of Economic
Analysis (BEA), surging AI-related business investment, and a 16% annualized increase in real
federal government spending. As policy uncertainty eased from its tariff-related spike and growth
remained resilient, labor data have also improved. Job openings and quits rates have stabilized.

The October payrolls reports exceeded expectations. The unemployment rate, according to
Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS), slipped to 4.1% from 4.5% in November 2025.
Stronger hiring alongside higher-than-expected October consumer price index (CPI) inflation revived
expectations for further Fed tightening, however, causing renewed sharp interest rate increases.
Yet, as discussed below, the recent strong growth reacceleration followed a period of softer, tariff-

shock-related economic conditions, and some of its driving forces are likely to moderate. Moreover,
inflation still appears disproportionately related to energy, tariffs, freight costs and increasing
AI-related electronics inflation. Tariffs have already started to fade, though, while diesel prices have
potential to decline once geopolitical conflicts resolve. In this context, there is a risk that higher
interest rates would not only overly restrain demand but would also delay investment in housing,

power generation and other capacity expansion needed to keep inflation in check.
Here are a few details underpinning this view:
• The strong Q3 real GDP growth acceleration followed just a 1.6% annualized pace over the preceding
three quarters, with consumer spending also up at a moderate 2.1% average rate. At about 2.1%
and 2.5% year-over-year (YoY) growth, respectively, neither GDP nor consumption growth appear
exceptional, according to BEA. The Chicago Fed National Activity Index, which aggregates information

from 85 economic indicators seems to concur—following three years of subtrend readings, it has
oscillated between slightly above- and below-trend growth territory for most of the year.
• Business surveys also suggest ongoing expansion but not booming growth. The National Federation
of Independent Business Small Business Optimism Index remains below its July level even as job
openings, hiring intentions, and plans to raise worker pay spiked in October. The October Institute

for Supply Management (ISM) manufacturing survey has remained a bright spot, with production and
employment numbers particularly robust. While the overall index is associated with roughly 2.7% real
GDP growth, according to the ISM, its services counterpart has lagged, with below-average readings
across new orders, production, and employment in October. As a result, the weighted average of the
two surveys remains way below levels seen in prior expansions (Exhibit 1), in line with the moderate

growth dynamics pervading the Fed’s October Beige Book, for example.
• According to BLS, hiring was unquestionably strong in October. Payrolls gained 165,000. Hiring in
previous months was revised up. The three-month average jobs growth shifted to 137,000 from just
28,000 in July. Unemployment dropped to 4.1%. Still, part of the improvement represents catch-up
from the hiring lull observed after the tariff shock and related spike in policy uncertainty (Exhibit 2).

Labor turnover remains low, with the quits rate unsurprisingly depressed given the sharp uptrend
in the average duration of unemployment, which increased in October to a five-year high (also a
historically high level).
• Labor supply has also surprised to the upside as employment strengthened, precluding excessive wage
growth outside specific areas of technically skilled labor scarcity. After cratering in the year following
the tariff shock, the labor force participation rate (LFPR) has meaningfully rebounded since July,

helping bring nearly 1.6 million people back looking for work, according to BLS. Indeed, the supply of
labor is partly endogenous to economic conditions—it rises when jobs availability and hiring improve,
and vice versa. Although the prime-age female LFPR is near recent records, a rebound in participation
rates for males age 20 to 54 toward their 1995 to 2000 highs would allow meaningful additional
employment growth before wage-related inflation pressures become an issue.

In itself, renewed hiring following the tariff-related hiatus therefore need not be inflationary. Higher
employment alongside rising labor force participation and solid productivity growth should help
contain unit labor costs. It would also help expand the supply side to better match resilient demand,
helping keep inflation in check and prolonging the expansion.
Investment Implications
We remain overweight Equities,
favor U.S. stocks and see potential

opportunities in broader market
participation and cyclical sectors.
Treasury yields appear attractive for
income generation and diversification
reasons.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
• Several supports to recent growth appear unsustainable. Base effects and midterm elections
results suggest a likely lessened growth impetus from AI investment. Faster inventory
accumulation, which significantly boosted Q3 real GDP, cannot continue indefinitely. Also, real
consumer spending barely inched higher in September following an August spike, according to
BEA. Indeed, consumer spending has increasingly outpaced income growth this year, sharply

eroding saving out of income from 6.2% in January to 3.8% in September, limiting the scope
for spending to keep outpacing income. Finally, while Q3 earnings have continued to positively
surprise across most sectors, margins may have limited scope of further expansion from
historically elevated levels. Together, based on our analysis, these factors point toward slower
but still healthy economic growth around 2.3% ahead, according to BofA Global Research.

• According to the BLS, “core” CPI inflation remained faster than desired in October, with
evidence of some broadening. The retreat in Brent oil from roughly $135 to $95 per barrel in
recent weeks has been encouraging, though elevated diesel prices and freight costs are still
spilling into other prices. Also, shelter seems to have run its disinflation course, accounting
for about a third of the October “core” CPI increase as excess rental inventory got absorbed.

Housing-services inflation is now close to its prepandemic average, providing a fading
disinflation tailwind.
All in all, growth is likely to moderate ahead, the tariff effect on inflation is dissipating,
and lower diesel prices would materially relieve near-term inflation pressure. Yet a set
of more structural supply constraints and potential inflation pressures remains to be
addressed. Rising electricity costs, infrastructure bottlenecks, skilled-labor shortages,

an aging population, housing costs and supply limitations, a wealthy segment of the
population relatively insensitive to inflation, disruptive trade and industrial policies, and
the resource demands associated with the AI-investment boom suggest a multitude of
potential inflation pressures if accommodated by persistent large fiscal deficits, excess
credit growth, a wage-cost spiral or unmitigated wealth effects. This strengthens the
case for measures to boost productive capacity and policies to better align nominal

spending with potential supply, if inflation is to sustainably return to target through
increased supply rather than higher reliance on Fed-induced demand suppression.
Restrictive interest rates may in fact delay the very supply response needed to alleviate
some of these inflation pressures.

economic conditions.

business demand for labor, and labor supply responded in kind.

4 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
From Earnings Strength to Earnings Durability
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
U.S. Equities have faced a series of crosscurrents in recent months. At the index level, stocks
have seemingly gone nowhere. The S&P 500 has yet to break out of the sideways trading
range that began in early June, rising less than 1% over that period. But that relatively flat

performance masks considerable movement beneath the surface. As enthusiasm around the
AI trade dominated through the summer, market leadership narrowed and breadth steadily
deteriorated. By the end of September, nearly 60% of S&P 500 constituents were more than
15% below their 52-week closing highs, suggesting a sizable correction was already unfolding
beneath the headline index. October and November brought another shift in leadership as
intensifying headwinds around the AI trade pressured mega-cap Technology stocks, while

previously lagging areas began to recover. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index fell as much
as 38% from its mid-June peak through early November and remains roughly 31% below that
high despite a recent partial recovery.
Against that volatile market backdrop, corporate fundamentals have told a much steadier
story. With roughly 91% of S&P 500 companies having reported as of November 13, Q3
earnings-per-share (EPS) growth is tracking around 32% YoY according to FactSet, which,

if sustained, would mark the third consecutive quarter of earnings growth above 25% and
the eighth consecutive quarter of double-digit growth. The magnitude of earnings growth in
recent quarters is unusual this far into an economic expansion and more closely resembles
the earnings rebounds typically seen following recessions. In fact, outside of the recoveries
following the 2000 Dot-com bubble peak, 2008/2009 Global Financial Crisis and 2020
pandemic, Q2 marked the fastest pace of earnings growth across the last three decades

(Exhibit 2). The underlying results have also remained strong, with revenue growth firmly in
double-digit territory and the Q3 net profit margin tracking its second-highest level since
FactSet began measuring the metric in 2009.
Exhibit 2: Earnings Growth Resembles a Post-Recession Rebound.

Yet equity markets have shown surprisingly little response to that earnings strength. According to

BofA Global Research, companies beating expectations on both earnings and sales outperformed
the S&P 500 by just 90 basis points the following day, compared with a historical average of 1.4
percentage points. Companies missing on both have traded roughly in line with the market, versus
an average historical underperformance of 2.5 percentage points. In turn, the resulting performance
gap between beats and misses is the narrowest since Q4 2000 and Q4 2001, following the March

2000 peak in the Dot-com bubble.1 The disconnect seems to be less about the strength of current
results than the increasingly high bar investors have set for what comes next.
Some moderation from here is almost inevitable. An increasingly difficult YoY comparison
base sets a high hurdle after several quarters of exceptional growth. BofA Global Research
expects S&P 500 EPS growth to slow from a projected 33% in 2026 to 12% in 2027, while
1 BofA Global Research, “Earnings Tracker: Week 4: 2021 beat rate, 2001 price action,” November 6, 2026.

Investment Implications
We continue to favor staying
invested while using periods of
market weakness to rebalance and
broaden portfolio exposure. As
earnings growth moderates and
market leadership becomes more
differentiated, investors should
remain highly diversified and
emphasize higher-quality exposure.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
bottom-up consensus remains somewhat more optimistic at 16%. That would represent a
meaningful deceleration, but it would not necessarily signal deterioration as double-digit
earnings growth would still leave the fundamental backdrop on solid footing.
The bigger question for investors though is what could turn that expected earnings growth
normalization into a sharper slowdown. Midterm election results have introduced a new source

of uncertainty about the AI investment cycle, as voters and newly elected Democratic governors
in several states at the center of the data center boom have supported greater restrictions on
development. That risk matters disproportionately for the earnings outlook, with Technology
and Energy accounting for roughly 75% of Q3 S&P 500 earnings growth (Exhibit 3A). BofA
Global Research estimates that a hypothetical 20% reduction in 2027 AI capital spending

could translate into 35% to 40% downside to AI semiconductor EPS and lower S&P 500 EPS
by roughly 7% to 8%, before accounting for secondary effects. So far, however, a slowdown
has yet to emerge in company guidance, with hyperscalers continuing to raise capital spending
plans. While recent market repricing has reflected some of that uncertainty, the durability of AI
investment and earnings remains an important factor in the broader earnings outlook.

For now, the conditions that typically accompany a broader earnings downturn remain largely
absent. Economic activity continues to expand at an above-trend pace, with the Atlanta Fed
GDPNow model tracking 3.5% annualized real gross domestic product growth for Q4. Consumer
spending also remains firm heading into the holiday season. Bank of America aggregated credit
and debit card spending per household rose 5.8% YoY in October, or 4.6% excluding gasoline.2

Although both measures moderated from September, they remain close to the strong growth
rates recorded since the spring. That demand resilience continues to show through corporate
earnings results. According to FactSet, 84% of S&P 500 companies have reported actual EPS
above mean estimates, well above the five-year average of 78%. And that strength extends well
beyond the largest contributors to index earnings growth, with nine of the 11 S&P 500 sectors

seeing 80% or more of reporting companies exceed consensus EPS estimates (Exhibit 3B) and
all sectors tracking positive earnings growth for 2026. While the earnings cycle may be moving
beyond its strongest phase, the broader backdrop does not yet point to a fundamental downturn.
For investors, a moderating earnings backdrop should place greater emphasis on where
earnings growth is coming from and how durable that growth ultimately proves. Greater

market dispersion should favor companies with healthy operating cash flows, strong balance
sheets and the ability to translate investment into sustainable returns. That applies particularly
to the AI buildout. A slower or more disciplined pace of capital spending would not necessarily
signal an end to the investment cycle but could help separate the companies and projects that
can ultimately deliver on their investment from those where expectations have moved too

far, too fast. That type of differentiation should become increasingly important as the market
moves into a more measured phase of the cycle.

6 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Mercantilist China Powers On: The Relentless Rise of Its Trade Surplus
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
And the boom continues. If investors have learned anything this year, it is that China’s not
ready to adjust its mercantilist growth model. Even in the face of higher U.S. tariffs and a
corresponding decline in exports to the U.S., China recorded a staggering $1T merchandise

trade surplus in just the first 10 months of this year. By the end of 2026, the country’s
trade surplus is on track to surpass 2025’s record and reach something closer to $1.3T3—or
triple the level at the start of the decade ($421 billion4) (Exhibit 4A).
Who is on the receiving end of all these exports? Hint: not the U.S., whose tariffs
taught the rest of the world that it can no longer count on America to absorb a significant
share of China’s exports. Per Exhibit 4B, as recently as 2019, the U.S. accounted for 20% of

exports from China. Today, that share is 10%, down sharply from 15% pre-Liberation Day.
That’s been a bitter pill to swallow for trading partners like the EU and Association of
Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN) countries, whose year-to-date deficits with China have
ballooned by 29% and 34% YoY, respectively. Germany, whose local manufacturing industry
continues to struggle under the weight of competition from the mainland, has seen its
deficit with China double in just the last year. It’s no surprise, then, that the chorus of

complaints against China’s mercantilist approach continues to swell beyond Washington.
From quotas on electric vehicle imports in the EU to anti-dumping investigations in India to
product-specific tariffs in Mexico, Brazil, and South Africa, the world is pushing back against
China and its well-subsidized industries.
Looking ahead to 2027, a full-blown tit-for-tat global trade war is far from our base
case. However, we wouldn’t be surprised to see more targeted tariffs, investigations, and

industrial subsidies from developed and emerging markets alike, especially in strategic
sectors like autos/parts, chemicals, steel and basic metals, and other manufactured goods.
In the U.S., despite the Democratic sweep at the midterm elections, tariffs are likely here to
stay via legal measures like Section 232 and Section 301.
For China, more pushback from the rest of the world could force the economy to finally
come to terms with persistently depressed domestic demand. Meanwhile, investment

in domestic manufacturing should continue in the U.S. and Europe. We favor the former
over the latter in portfolios because the diversified nature of the U.S. economy, existing
protectionist measures, and tilt toward services and innovation leave growth relatively less
exposed to competition in manufacturing.
Exhibit 4: Taking Stock of China’s #1 Growth Engine.
A) China On Track for Another Record Annual Trade Surplus. B) Breaking it Down: Who Receives China's Exports?

well-diversified services-led economy
should be relatively less exposed to
China’s manufactured goods exports.

China on U.S. technology supremacy is
an ongoing key risk to watch.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Chicago Fed National Activity Index is a monthly index designed to gauge overall economic activity and related inflationary pressure.
Consumer Price Index is a measure of the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services. Indexes are available for the
U.S. and various geographic areas.
National Federation of Independent Business Small Business Optimism Index is a monthly composite economic indicator that measures the health, confidence, and expectations of small

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.
Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and
investment advisory services, including the type of advice and assistance provided, the fees charged, and the rights and obligations of the parties. It is important to understand the differences,
particularly when determining which service or services to select. For more information about these services and their differences, speak with your Merrill financial advisor.

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Will
the
same
causes
produce
the
same
effects?
In
other
words,
will
the
war
in
Iran
and
the
resulting
surge
in
oil
and
gas
prices
lead
to
an
inflationary
shock
comparable
to
that
seen
in
2022
?
Will
their
negative
effects
on
growth
be
the
same
as
those
of
the
war
in
Ukraine
and
the
subsequent
energy
shock?
We
have
selected
a
set
of
indicators
to
track
the
impact
of
this
new
energy
shock
—
caused
by

the
war
in
the
Middle
East
—
on
activity
and
prices
in
the
Eurozone,
the
United
States,
oil
and
gas
markets
and

countries,
and
to
see
how
much
the
current
situation
resembles
that
of
2022
at
the
outbreak
of
the
conflict
in
Ukraine
.
This
update
marks
the
end
of
the
publication
of
this
graphical
and
commented
dashboard
.
The
cyclical
monitoring
continues
in
our
other
publications
.
Globally,
based
on
the
data
available
up
to
October
2026
,

the
2026
energy
shock
has
had
a
less
pronounced
impact
on
activity
and
inflation
than
that
of
2022
.
The
deterioration
in
business
climate
surveys
following
the
outbreak
of
the
conflict
in
Iran
was,
overall,
less
severe,
and
an
improvement
followed
fairly
quickly
,
driven
by
the
positive
impact
of
the
AI
boom
.
Household
confidence
has
been
more
affected
by
the
rise
in
energy
prices,
without
benefiting
from
the
cushioning
effect
of

boom
.
On

-

have

since

in

and

.

.

:

ECONOMICS & STRATEGY 
Thailand Chartbook: K-shaped divergence 
17 November 2026 
go.dbs.com/insightsdirect/macro/home

groupresc@dbs.com 
• The uptick in Thai GDP growth to 2.9% yoy in 3Q26 from 
2.4% yoy in 2Q masked a widening K-shaped divergence 
in growth. 
• The strong technology-led investment and export cycle 
will continue at a sustainable pace in 2027. 
• Private consumption will stay constrained by high debt, 
a weak labour market, and muted sentiment. 
• Fiscal space to sustain repeated stimulus measures and 
support household spending is narrowing.  
• Soft tourism is set for a seasonal recovery in 4Q26 and 
a gradual improvement in 2027. 
• We expect the BOT to remain on an extended pause 
throughout 2027, supporting an uneven economy. 
• Forecast implications: We expect real GDP growth and 
headline inflation to ease to 2.0% and 1.0%, 
respectively, in 2027, from revised forecasts of 2.4% 
and 2.1% in 2026 (2.1% and 1.7% previously).

Real GDP growth picked up to 2.9% 
yoy in 3Q26 from an upwardly revised 
2.4% in 2Q. However, the K-shaped 
divergence in growth widened. We 
forecast growth to ease to 2.0% in 
2027 from an adjusted 2.4% in 2026.

Net trade contributed positively in 3Q, 
as export expansion outpaced robust 
import growth, driven by 
supercharged demand for artificial 
intelligence (AI)-related electronics.

Overall growth gained momentum in 
3Q, but domestic demand was highly 
uneven. Investment surged, while 
private and government consumption 
were sluggish, although household 
spending edged up.

Higher GDP growth in 3Q was notably 
driven by stronger technology-
related industrial expansion, while 
the agriculture and services 
sustained their yoy growth.

primary driver.

Monthly data showed a sizeable jump 
in machinery and equipment 
investment in September. While this 
increase is unlikely to be sustained, 
we expect it to stay robust given the 
strong investment project pipeline.

Surging investment has been led 
notably by machinery and equipment 
outlays, underpinned by rising capital 
goods imports and local machinery 
sales relating to electronics.

Strong investment applications in the 
first three quarters of 2026 were 
driven mainly by the digital sector, 
which doubled from a year earlier, 
supported by favourable policies, and 
robust foreign investor interest.

Charts of the week: Diverging volatility

Strong earnings should resolve the bond and equity market divergence; French political risks remain largely contained

Editorial 
Will earnings resolve the bond-equity split?  
Strategy summary: Bond and equity market volatility has 
sharply diverged. Elevated bond volatility and political risks in 
the US and Europe warrant near-term caution, prompting us to 
lock in tactical gains in semiconductors and Taiwan equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turned negative for the first 
time since March 2025, signalling near-term equity volatility. 
We stay invested in a diversified allocation slightly overweight 
equities and prefer to navigate the upcoming results and macro 
events before adding high-beta exposure within equities. 
Consensus estimates point to a c. 31% y/y rise in S&P500 Q3 
earnings, driven by technology-sector earnings growth of c. 
67%. Earnings have beaten lofty estimates in H1 as the AI 
build-out exceeds expectations. This favours tech-led equities 
in the medium term. In Europe, French political and fiscal risks 
cloud the EUR outlook, but regional bank equities remain 
attractive on robust profitability, capital strength and valuations. 
Divergence between equities and bonds. Equity markets 
signal calm, with the S&P500 index near record highs and its 
volatility index (VIX) low at 15, while the US government bond 
volatility index (MOVE) is close to its highest since March. Bond 
stress reflects two-decade-high long yields amid strong growth, 
oil close to USD 100/bbl fuelling near-term inflation, fiscal 
uncertainty and crowded investor short positioning vulnerable 
to abrupt unwinds. High bond volatility could spill into equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turns negative. For the 
first time since March 2025, the model score has turned 
negative, driven by deteriorating equity market breadth and 
above-average risk aversion. History shows global equity 
volatility rises most in the month after the score dips to -1. 
However, equities still outperformed bonds over the following 
1-12 months, on average, despite higher market risk. 
Taking some chips off the table; locking in gains: Rising 
bond volatility, the negative stock-bond model signal, weak 
equity breadth and approaching US mid-term elections warrant 
near-term caution. US small-caps remain about 9% below 
August’s all-time highs even as large-caps reach new records. 
The macro uncertainty prompted us to lock in gains in 
semiconductor and Taiwan equity opportunistic ideas. 
Strong Q3 earnings season likely to overcome near-term 
challenges. Strong US and Asian earnings should ultimately 
resolve the equity-bond divergence in favour of stocks. 
Consensus S&P500 Q3 earnings growth has risen to 30.6% 
y/y, while technology is tracking 66.5%, driven by the AI 
infrastructure buildout. AI investment plans, monetisation and 
funding needs are key metrics to watch. Record memory-chip 
profits and higher long-term targets from AI leaders support 
staying long technology-led risk assets and using near-term 
volatility to scale into preferred sectors. Also, US equities have 
historically performed strongly in the 12 months after mid-term 
elections, particularly when the opposition regains control of the 
House and the Senate – as seems increasingly likely. 
Medium-term constructive on equities. Beyond Q3 earnings, 
we expect US earnings to rise 15-20% next year. Almost half of 
the estimated USD 420 per share of S&P500 earnings in 2027 
should come from tech-related sectors: information technology, 
communication services and consumer discretionary. We also 
favour US, Europe and Japan financial sectors as solid growth 
and higher rates lift lending income, while the AI buildout boosts 
advisory fees from IPOs and M&As. However, another 30-
50bps rise in long-term bond yields, without earnings growth 
upgrades, could be a headwind for equities. Next week’s US 
inflation data for September is the next catalyst for bond yields. 
French uncertainty challenges near-term EUR view; 
continue to see opportunities in Euro area bank equities. 
Rising French political and fiscal risks have largely remained 
confined to France, with its bond yield spread versus German 
peers surging to the widest since the 2011-12 Euro area crisis. 
Nevertheless, EUR/USD has fallen around 4% since mid-
August, and our three-month forecast of 1.15 is under review. 
Tighter financial conditions and political uncertainty in France 
and Spain raises the risk of an ECB rate hold. Euro area bank 
equities remain attractive on profitability, capital strength and 
valuations, subject to sovereign-spread and funding-cost risks.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the market implications of France's political and 
fiscal uncertainty?   
Our view: France’s political and fiscal uncertainty remain near-
term headwinds for European assets. We are placing our three-
month EUR/USD forecast (1.15) under review for a potential 
downward adjustment, while retaining our 12-month forecast at 
1.18 as broader Euro area spillovers remain limited. Our base 
case remains one more ECB hike in December followed by a 
prolonged pause, although tighter financial conditions increase 
the risk of a delay. For equities, we retain Euro area banks as 
an Opportunistic idea, supported by strong profitability, capital 
buffers and reasonable valuations. For bank bonds, we prefer a 
subordinated capital structure to senior debt. 
Rationale: France’s 10-year government bond yield has risen amid 
renewed political and fiscal concerns. This has tightened financial 
conditions and added to the near-term EUR risk premium. Together 
with elevated US yields, this raises downside risks to our three-
month EUR/USD view. However, the stress has not yet developed 
into broad Euro area contagion, and recent expectations of progress 
on France’s 2027 budget have provided some relief. Broader Euro 
area growth and labour market fundamentals also remain relatively 
resilient, supporting our unchanged 12-month EUR/USD view. 
For the ECB, higher sovereign yields are already delivering some 
monetary tightening. Markets have consequently pared expectations 
of additional ECB hikes. We continue to expect one more hike in 
December, given elevated near-term inflation, but persistent fiscal 
or political stress in France and Spain could delay further tightening. 
For Euro area banks, wider sovereign spreads are likely to raise 
funding costs and pressure bond portfolios, while fewer ECB hikes 
would moderate further gains in net interest income. Nevertheless, 
profitability remains strong, capital buffers are healthy and price-
to-book valuations remain reasonable, with returns on equity also 
improving. We, therefore, maintain Euro area banks as an 
Opportunistic idea, while monitoring sovereign spreads, funding 
costs and any deterioration in loan-loss provisions.  
On the bond side, European bank junior subordinated bond prices 
fell by an average of 3-5 cash points before rebounding, with French 
bank subordinated bonds underperforming those of other European 
peers. As fundamentals remain strong, we view pullbacks as 
opportunities to add and maintain our preference for a subordinated 
capital structure over senior debt for European bank bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist  
 Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the likelihood of elevated long-end government 
bond yields evolving into a systemic risk for the financial sector 
and public finances? 
Our view: We believe systemic risk remains a tail risk rather 
than a base case in the near term. The probability would rise 
materially if rollover stress, foreign demand withdrawal and 
private credit deterioration occur in combination. 
Rationale: Higher bond yields worsen fiscal deficits and drive higher 
issuance requirements, exerting further upward pressure on yields. 
The resulting tighter financial conditions weigh on economic growth. 
This feedback loop is most acute in Developed Markets (DMs) where 
debt burdens are already elevated. For instance, projected 2026 
debt-to-GDP ratios stand at 204% for Japan, 138% for Italy, 126% 
for the US, 118% for France and 104% for the UK. Countries with 
shorter average debt maturities face the sharpest near-term 
repricing risk as maturing obligations are refinanced at current 
rates. Meanwhile, evidence suggests that a positive equity-bond 
correlation increases the sensitivity of yields to bond supply.  
Taking the US as an example, there has been no buyers’ strike on 
US government bonds yet, though foreign demand has softened, 
narrowing the margin of safety. The US government has shifted 
towards shorter-dated issuance to manage funding costs (reducing 
near-term expenses, but concentrating rollover risk). Economic 
resilience remains evident, underpinned by corporate confidence 
in AI-driven investment. The key question is whether this 
expansion can persist long enough to stabilise the fiscal trajectory. 
Within the financial sector, corporate balance sheets remain 
strong, while High-Yield (HY) bond and leveraged loan default rates 
remain contained, though this reflects the lag between rate rises and 
credit deterioration, not immunity. Following the failure of the Silicon 
Valley Bank, liquidity discipline among banks has improved, but 
duration mismatches persist.  
In private credit, default rates are trending upward while the asset 
class has come under continued scrutiny, with negative headlines 
and a rise in redemptions triggering fund gating at some private 
credit managers since Q1 2026. Hence, continued monitoring of 
default rates, redemptions and potential stress events in private 
credit remains essential. That said, higher rates could buoy 
medium-term returns and distribution yields in the asset class, 
as the majority of direct lending loans have a floating-rate structure. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

Most G8 economies have government debt-to-GDP 
ratios exceeding 100%

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for the US Q3 2026 earnings season, 
especially for the banking sector? 
Our view: We expect another quarter of robust US earnings 
growth to support our Overweight stance on US equities. We 
see major US banks, which begin reporting next week, also 
delivering healthy results, reinforcing our Overweight view on 
the US financial sector. 
Rationale: Consensus forecasts S&P500 Q3 earnings growth of 
30.6% y/y, up from 27.6% at the start of July. Energy is expected to 
post the strongest growth at 114.7%, supported by elevated oil 
prices amid ongoing geopolitical tensions. Information technology 
(66.5%) and communication services (49.0%) remain key 
earnings drivers, benefiting from sustained AI-related demand and 
investment. The broader outlook remains supportive, with full-year 
earnings growth projected at 35.8% in 2026 and 15.6% in 2027. 
For banks, the recent underperformance amid concerns over higher 
long-term bond yields has, in our view, improved the forward-looking 
risk-reward profile. Over the medium term, strengthening loan 
growth and resilient credit quality should continue to support solid 
fundamentals. Capital markets activity also remains constructive, 
with industry leaders well-positioned to benefit from their strong 
investment banking and trading franchises. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst 
  How could upcoming semiconductor manufacturing and 
equipment earnings reshape the AI capex outlook? 
Our view: We maintain our AI capex outlook as robust demand 
and ongoing supply constraints support a multi-year expansion 
cycle. In the near term, however, we take a more prudent stance 
on semiconductors amid an expected rise in equity volatility. 
Rationale: Recent memory industry guidance points to sustained 
investment in advanced chip manufacturing, with customer 
commitments extending through 2031. Upcoming earnings should 
provide visibility into capacity expansion plans. This supports our AI 
capex outlook, which we will monitor through the earnings season.  
That said, we expect equity volatility to rise in the near term amid 
elevated long-term yields and macroeconomic uncertainty. Recent 
media reports on AI lab revenues have also led to doubts over 
monetisation and the ability to sustain elevated capex spending. We 
thus took profit on our Global Semiconductors (8.1% return from 9-
Sep-26 to 2-Oct-26) and MSCI Taiwan (10.0% return from 9-Jul-26 to 
2-Oct-26) Opportunistic ideas following strong performance. This does 
not reflect a deterioration in fundamentals, but rather a need to be 
prudent on high-beta exposure. We remain constructive on the AI 
theme over the long term. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist
